### C.2. Tool Interfaces and Minimal Prototype

The PMG experimental layer exposes two tool interfaces to the agents:
the Architect utilizes submit_topology to propose candidate
architectures, and the Auditor utilizes inject_chaos to introduce
numerical perturbations into the latest topology.
Table [15](#A3.T15 "Table 15 ‣ C.2. Tool Interfaces and Minimal Prototype ‣ Appendix C PMG Physical Mapping Supplement ‣ Grounding SWE-Agent Decisions in Architecture-0 Design: Navigating Unknown Unknowns through Physical Mapping")
outlines their input/output boundaries.

|  Interface |  Input Fields |  Output Fields |  Exceptions & Usage |
|----|----|----|----|
|  submit_topology |  topology_yaml: YAML/JSON string matching the topological template. |  mapper_status, visible_ledger, ledger_aligned_bill, profiler_breakdown, nac, collisions, mapper_feedback, cycle_info |  Errors or UNMAPPABLE_TOPOLOGY are returned for non-objects, invalid node types, missing edge references, or unbounded loops. Used to submit candidate architectures. |
|  inject_chaos |  chaos_yaml: YAML/JSON string containing chaos_target, attack, and override_parameters. |  Alongside standard mapping results, it returns chaos_target, attack, and applied_overrides. |  Returns an error if no topology exists, the target node is missing, or the injected fields are non-numeric/unsupported by the profile. Used to observe physical projection shifts under load perturbations. |

Table 15. Input and Output Boundaries of PMG Tool Interfaces

The following code snippet abstracts the execution chain of the PMG
minimal prototype. While the full implementation includes granular error
handling, graph propagation, and reporting logic, this snippet preserves
the core boundaries required for the S2P mapping trunk.

1 def submit_topology(case_data, topology_text):

2 topology = load_structured_text(topology_text, "\<submit_topology\>")

3 topology.setdefault("case_id", case_data\["case_id"\])

4 topology.setdefault("assumptions", {})

5 topology.setdefault("nodes", \[\])

6 topology.setdefault("edges", \[\])

7 result = run_mapper(case_data, topology)

8 return build_agent_facing_view(result)

9

10 def run_mapper(case_data, topology):

11 case_data = normalize_case(case_data)

12 visible_ledger, hidden_ledger, \_ =
split_visible_vs_hidden_constraints(case_data)

13 validate_topology(topology, case_data\["case_id"\])

14 adjacency, reverse_adjacency, edge_map = build_graph(topology)

15 propagation = resolve_node_workloads(topology, adjacency,
reverse_adjacency, edge_map)

16

17 node_bills = \[\]

18 for node in topology\["nodes"\]:

19 workload = propagation.resolved_workloads.get(node\["node_id"\], {})

20 node = {\*\*node, "workload": {\*\*workload, \*\*node.get("workload",
{})}}

21 node_bills.append(bill_node(node, topology.get("assumptions", {}),
hidden_ledger, NODE_PROFILE_LIBRARY))

22

23 aggregate_bill = aggregate_node_bills(node_bills,
topology.get("assumptions", {}))

24 ledger_aligned_bill = project_to_ledger_metrics(aggregate_bill)

25 nac = compute_nac_table(ledger_aligned_bill, visible_ledger,
hidden_ledger)

26 collisions = detect_collisions(node_bills, nac)

27 status = "PASS" if not collisions else "COLLISION"

28 return {

29 "mapper_status": status,

30 "node_bills": node_bills,

31 "ledger_aligned_bill": ledger_aligned_bill,

32 "nac": nac,

33 "collisions": collisions,

34 }

### C.3. Resource Profiles and Constants

PMG evaluates resources across stable, first-order dimensions: CPU,
Memory, Network, and Storage. To ensure that constants and formulas are
not arbitrarily generated or manipulated by the LLM, the internal mapper
maintains a registry of reference sources.
Table [16](#A3.T16 "Table 16 ‣ C.3. Resource Profiles and Constants ‣ Appendix C PMG Physical Mapping Supplement ‣ Grounding SWE-Agent Decisions in Architecture-0 Design: Navigating Unknown Unknowns through Physical Mapping")
summarizes the primary authoritative sources and their specific
applications within the mapping logic.

|  Source Category |  Representative Sources |  Application in Constraints or Formulas |
|----|----|----|
|  Universal Resource Observability |  USE Method ([Gregg, 2013](#bib.bib14)); Google Borg Resource Management ([Verma et al., 2015](#bib.bib37)) |  Adopts CPU, Memory, Network, and Storage as the first-order dimensions for system-level resource ledgers. |
|  Queuing & Throughput Modeling |  Quantitative System Performance ([Lazowska et al., 1984](#bib.bib20)) |  Supports baseline linear service approximations, throughput/latency inferences, and calibrates metrics like MAX_QPS and P99_LATENCY_MS. |
|  Unit Conversions |  NIST SI Units Guide ([Thompson and Taylor, 2008](#bib.bib36)) |  Enforces deterministic conversions for bytes, bits, bandwidth, and throughput units. |
|  Cloud Resource Specs & Pricing |  Lambda Pricing ([Amazon Web Services, 2026d](#bib.bib6)), Quotas ([Amazon Web Services, 2026e](#bib.bib7)); API Gateway Pricing ([Amazon Web Services, 2026a](#bib.bib3)); EC2 / EBS Specs ([Amazon Web Services, 2026c](#bib.bib5); [Amazon Web Services, 2026b](#bib.bib4)) |  Supports upper bounds for budgets, function memory, instance limits, network bandwidth, and disk IOPS. |
|  Middleware & Runtime Constraints |  Redis Memory Optimization ([Redis, 2026](#bib.bib33)); Linux TCP Buffers ([Linux man-pages project, 2026](#bib.bib23)); MySQL System Variables ([Oracle Corporation, 2026b](#bib.bib27)); Java Thread Stack ([Oracle Corporation, 2026a](#bib.bib26)) |  Supports implicit resource bounds such as cache memory overheads, TCP long-lived connections, DB connection pools, and thread stack allocations. |
|  Prototype Calibration |  A2TA internal prototype calibration |  Defines local calibration metrics for experiment defaults, migration windows, project cycles, and consistency windows. |

Table 16. Categories and Sources for PMG Implicit Constants and Formulas

Table [17](#A3.T17 "Table 17 ‣ C.3. Resource Profiles and Constants ‣ Appendix C PMG Physical Mapping Supplement ‣ Grounding SWE-Agent Decisions in Architecture-0 Design: Navigating Unknown Unknowns through Physical Mapping")
outlines representative Resource Profile formulas utilized in the PMG
prototype. Variables correspond to implementation parameters: $`I`$
(Instances), $`Q`$ (Query Throughput), $`C`$ (Connections), $`S`$
(Average Payload Size), and $`H`$ (Hours per month).

|  Formula Type |  Calculation Form |  Applicable Profile |  Source Category |
|----|----|----|----|
|  Base Value + Linear Term |  $`R=B\cdot I+X\cdot k`$. (e.g., in connection memory estimation, $`B`$ is base memory, $`X`$ is connection count, $`k`$ is per-connection coefficient). |  Stateless Service, Relational DB |  Throughput Modeling, Runtime Constraints. |
|  Request Load to Bandwidth |  NETWORK_MBPS = $`Q\cdot S\cdot 8/1024`$, where $`S`$ is in KB. |  api_gateway |  Unit Conversions, Cloud Specs. |
|  Serverless Monthly Cost |  MONTHLY_COST = $`(Q_{h}H/10^{6})\cdot p_{r}+Q_{h}H\cdot(M/1024)\cdot(D/1000)\cdot p_{g}`$ |  lambda_function |  Cloud Pricing, Lambda Parameters. |
|  NAC Normalization |  $`\mathrm{NAC}=(\hat{x}-L)/L`$; Direction reversed for MIN\_\* metrics. |  All Ledger Metrics |  Ledger Thresholds & Formulas. |

Table 17. Representative Resource Profile Formulas in PMG

Table [18](#A3.T18 "Table 18 ‣ C.3. Resource Profiles and Constants ‣ Appendix C PMG Physical Mapping Supplement ‣ Grounding SWE-Agent Decisions in Architecture-0 Design: Navigating Unknown Unknowns through Physical Mapping")
presents the ledger visibility strategies for three core cases.

[TABLE]

Table 18. Ledger Visibility Strategies for the Three Core Deep-Dive
Cases
